### North America

Main articles: [Tesla Fremont Factory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tesla_Fremont_Factory), [Gigafactory Nevada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gigafactory_Nevada), [Gigafactory New York](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gigafactory_New_York), [Gigafactory Texas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gigafactory_Texas), and [Gigafactory Mexico](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gigafactory_Mexico)



New [Tesla Model S](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tesla_Model_S) cars at the [Tesla Fremont Factory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tesla_Fremont_Factory) in 2012

Tesla was founded in [San Carlos, California](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Carlos,_California) in 2003.[^cite_note-259] In 2008, the company opened its first production facility at a former [Chevrolet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chevrolet) dealership in [Menlo Park, California](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Menlo_Park,_California). The original roadster was assembled inside the service bays until 2012 and used the company's showroom as a retail store.[^cite_note-inmenlo-Gulker-31] Another retail store was opened in Los Angeles the same year.[^cite_note-260] In 2010, Tesla moved its corporate headquarters and opened a powertrain development facility in [Palo Alto](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palo_Alto).[^cite_note-261]

Tesla's first major assembly plant occupies the former [NUMMI](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NUMMI) plant in Fremont, California, known as the [Tesla Fremont Factory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tesla_Fremont_Factory). The factory was originally opened by [General Motors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_Motors) in 1962, and then operated by NUMMI, a joint venture of GM and [Toyota](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Toyota) from 1984.[^cite_note-262] The joint venture ended when [GM entered bankruptcy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_Motors_Chapter_11_reorganization) in 2009. In 2010, Toyota agreed to sell the plant to Tesla at a significant discount.[^cite_note-Future-37]



[Gigafactory Nevada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gigafactory_Nevada) in 2019

Tesla's first purpose-built facility was opened in Nevada in 2016. [Gigafactory Nevada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gigafactory_Nevada) produces the Powerwall,[^cite_note-Powerwall-246] battery cells in partnership with Panasonic,[^cite_note-263] Model 3 drivetrains,[^cite_note-264] and the Tesla Semi.[^cite_note-Hidalgo_March_2,_20232-265] The factory received substantial subsidies ([abatements](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tax_abatement) and [credits](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tax_credit)) from the local and state government, that, in exchange for opening in their jurisdiction, allowed Tesla to operate essentially tax-free for 10 years,[^cite_note-266] later extended to 20 years in exchange for expanding the factory to add a production line for the Tesla Semi and add additional battery manufacturing capacity.[^cite_note-Hidalgo_March_2,_20232-265]

As part of the acquisition of SolarCity in 2016, Tesla gained control of [Gigafactory New York](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gigafactory_New_York) in Buffalo on the site of a former [Republic Steel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Republic_Steel) plant. The state of New York spent cash to build and equip the factory through the [Buffalo Billion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Buffalo_Billion) program.[^cite_note-267][^cite_note-268] In 2017, the factory started production of the [Tesla Solar Roof](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tesla_Solar_Roof),[^cite_note-UpAndRunning-249] but faced multiple production challenges. Since 2020, Tesla has also assembled Superchargers in New York. The plant has been criticized for offering little economic benefit for the state funding.[^cite_note-Bykowicz_&_Mann_2023-269]

In 2018, Tesla assembled [tension fabric buildings](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tension_fabric_building) at the [Fremont](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fremont,_California) plant to meet production goals of 5,000 cars produced a month. The structure was assembled in two weeks and measured 53 feet high, 150 feet wide, and 900 feet long.[^cite_note-clearspan.com-270]



[Gigafactory Texas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gigafactory_Texas) in 2022

On July 23, 2020, Tesla picked Austin, Texas, as the site of its fifth Gigafactory, since then known as [Gigafactory Texas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gigafactory_Texas).[^cite_note-electrek_texas_2021-271] Giga Texas is the only factory that produces the Tesla Cybertruck and produces Model Y cars for the Eastern United States. On December 1, 2021, Tesla announced it relocated its legal headquarters from Palo Alto to the Gigafactory Texas site in Austin.[^cite_note-272] However, Tesla has retained the Palo Alto building. On April 7, 2022, Tesla celebrated the opening of Gigafactory Texas in a public event.[^cite_note-auto1-79]

Tesla acquired a former [JC Penney](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/JC_Penney) distribution center near [Lathrop, California](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lathrop,_California) in 2021 to build the "Megafactory" to manufacture the [Megapack](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Megapack), the company's large-scale energy storage product.[^cite_note-MR_Bulletin-273][^cite_note-Lathrop_MF-274] The location opened in 2022.

Tesla announced in February it would open a new global engineering headquarters in Palo Alto, moving into a corporate campus once owned by [Hewlett Packard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/HP_Inc.), located a couple of miles from Tesla's former headquarters building.[^cite_note-LA-Times-2023-02-23-87]

Tesla has announced plans to open a [Gigafactory Mexico](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gigafactory_Mexico), the company's sixth Gigafactory, near [Monterrey](40455.md), Mexico. However, as of July 2024, the company had placed construction on hold until after the [2024 United States presidential election](59568546.md) because former President Trump has pledged to add tariffs on cars made in Mexico.[^cite_note-275]

### Europe

Main articles: [Tesla facilities in Tilburg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tesla_facilities_in_Tilburg), [Tesla Automation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tesla_Automation), and [Gigafactory Berlin-Brandenburg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gigafactory_Berlin-Brandenburg)



Gigafactory Berlin in July 2023

Tesla opened its first European store in June 2009 in [London](17867.md).[^cite_note-276] Tesla's European headquarters are in [the Netherlands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Netherlands),[^cite_note-277] part of a group of [Tesla facilities in Tilburg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tesla_facilities_in_Tilburg), including the company's European Distribution Centre.[^cite_note-278]

In late 2016, Tesla acquired German engineering firm [Grohmann Engineering](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grohmann_Engineering) as a new division dedicated to helping Tesla increase the automation and effectiveness of its manufacturing process.[^cite_note-279] After winding down existing contracts with other manufacturers, the renamed [Tesla Automation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tesla_Automation) now works exclusively on Tesla projects and is expanding its operations in Germany through the acquisition of other manufacturing specialists.[^cite_note-280]

Tesla announced its plans to build a car and battery factory in Europe in 2016.[^cite_note-281] Several countries campaigned to be the host,[^cite_note-282] and eventually Germany was chosen in November 2019.[^cite_note-283] On March 22, 2022, Tesla's first European Gigafactory named [Gigafactory Berlin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gigafactory_Berlin-Brandenburg)[^cite_note-284][^cite_note-Graupner-2022-285] opened with planned capacity to produce 500,000 electric vehicles annually as well as batteries for the cars.[^cite_note-Graupner-2022-285]

### Asia

Main article: [Gigafactory Shanghai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gigafactory_Shanghai)



Tesla store in Tokyo, the first in Asia

[^cite_note-286]

Tesla opened its first showroom in [Asia](689.md) in [Tokyo](30057.md), Japan, in October 2010.[^cite_note-287]

In July 2018, Tesla signed an agreement with Chinese authorities to build a factory in [Shanghai](27643.md), China, which was Tesla's first Gigafactory outside the United States.[^cite_note-288] The factory building was finished in August 2019, and the initial [Tesla Model 3s](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tesla_Model_3) were in production from Gigafactory Shanghai in October 2019.[^cite_note-cnbc20191023-251] In 2024, China accounted for 21% of Tesla sales revenue, and was the second-largest market for Tesla after the United States, which accounted for 48% of its sales.[^cite_note-289] Tesla also sold 37% of its cars in China in 2024.[^cite_note-290]

Tesla expressed interest in 2023 in expanding to India and perhaps building a future Gigafactory in the country.[^cite_note-291] The company established a legal presence in the nation in 2021 and plans to open an office in [Pune](164634.md) starting in October 2023.[^cite_note-292]

## Partners

### Panasonic



[Panasonic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Panasonic) Energy president Naoto Noguchi presents Tesla executive [J. B. Straubel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/J._B._Straubel) with [lithium-ion cells](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lithium-ion_cells).

In January 2010, Tesla and battery cell maker [Panasonic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Panasonic) announced that they would together develop [nickel-based](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nickel#Applications) [lithium-ion battery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lithium-ion_battery) cells for electric vehicles.[^cite_note-293] Beginning in 2010, Panasonic invested \$30 million for a multi-year collaboration on new battery cells designed specifically for electric vehicles.[^cite_note-294] In July 2014, Panasonic reached a basic agreement with Tesla to participate in battery production at Giga Nevada.[^cite_note-295] Tesla and Panasonic also collaborated on the manufacturing and production of photovoltaic (PV) cells and modules at the Giga New York factory in Buffalo, New York.[^cite_note-UpAndRunning-249] The partnership started in mid-2017 and ended in early 2020, before Panasonic exited the solar business entirely in January 2021.[^cite_note-296][^cite_note-Reuters-2021-297]

In March 2021, the outgoing CEO of Panasonic stated that the company plans to reduce its reliance on Tesla as its battery partnership evolves.[^cite_note-298]

### Other current partners

Tesla has long-term contracts in place for [lithium](17561.md) supply. In September 2020, Tesla signed a sales agreement with [Piedmont Lithium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Piedmont_Lithium) to buy high-purity lithium ore for up to ten years,[^cite_note-mining20200928-299] specifically to supply "[spodumene](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spodumene) concentrate from Piedmont's North Carolina [mineral deposit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mineral_deposit)".[^cite_note-300] In 2022, Tesla contracted for 110,000 tonnes of [spodumene](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spodumene) concentrate over four years from the [Core Lithium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Core_Lithium)'s lithium mine in the [Northern Territory](21638.md) of Australia.[^cite_note-pvmag20221013-301]

Tesla also has a range of minor partnerships, for instance, working with [Airbnb](32058290.md) and hotel chains to install destination chargers at selected locations.[^cite_note-302] Another minor partnership would include Tesla's partnership with insurance company, [Lemonade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lemonade,_Inc.), to launch an autonomous car insurance.[^cite_note-303]

### Former partners

#### Daimler



The [Mercedes-Benz B-Class Electric Drive](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mercedes-Benz_B-Class_Electric_Drive) used a Tesla-supplied battery pack.

[^cite_note-304]

[Daimler](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Daimler_AG) and Tesla began working together in late 2007. On May 19, 2009, Daimler bought a stake of less than 10% in Tesla for a reported \$50 million.[^cite_note-techcrunch1-305][^cite_note-306] As part of the collaboration, Herbert Kohler, vice-president of E-Drive and Future Mobility at Daimler, took a Tesla board seat.[^cite_note-TN_BOD_2012-307] On July 13, 2009, Daimler sold 40% of its acquisition to Aabar, an investment company controlled by the [International Petroleum Investment Company](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Petroleum_Investment_Company) owned by the government of [Abu Dhabi](18950756.md).[^cite_note-308] In October 2014, Daimler sold its remaining holdings for a reported \$780 million.[^cite_note-309]

Tesla supplied battery packs for [Freightliner Trucks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Freightliner_Trucks) in 2010.[^cite_note-310][^cite_note-311] The company also built electric-powertrain components for the [Mercedes-Benz A-Class E-Cell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mercedes-Benz_A-Class_E-Cell), with 500 cars planned to be built for trial in Europe beginning in September 2011.[^cite_note-312][^cite_note-313] Tesla produced and co-developed the [Mercedes-Benz B250e](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mercedes-Benz_B-Class_Electric_Drive)'s powertrain, which ended production in 2017.[^cite_note-314] The electric motor was rated 134 hp (100 kW) and 230 pound force-feet (310 N⋅m), with a 36 kWh (130 MJ) battery. The vehicle had a driving range of 200 km (124 mi) with a top speed of 150 km/h (93 mph).[^cite_note-315] Daimler division [Smart](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Smart_\(marque\)) produced the [Smart ED2](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Smart_electric_drive) cars from 2009 to 2012 which had a 14-kilowatt-hour (50 MJ) [lithium-ion battery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lithium-ion_battery) from Tesla.[^cite_note-316][^cite_note-317]

#### Toyota



[Toyota RAV4 EV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Toyota_RAV4_EV), which used a Tesla-supplied battery and [powertrain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Powertrain) components

In May 2010, Tesla and [Toyota](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Toyota) announced a deal in which Tesla purchased the former [NUMMI](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NUMMI) factory from Toyota for \$42 million, Toyota purchased \$50 million in Tesla stock, and the two companies collaborated on an electric vehicle.[^cite_note-Future-37]

In July 2010, the companies announced they would work together on a second generation [Toyota RAV4 EV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Toyota_RAV4_EV).[^cite_note-318] The vehicle was unveiled at the October 2010 [Los Angeles Auto Show](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Los_Angeles_Auto_Show) and 35 pilot vehicles were built for a demonstration and evaluation program that ran through 2011. Tesla supplied the lithium metal-oxide battery and other powertrain components[^cite_note-319][^cite_note-320] based on components from the Roadster.[^cite_note-321]

The production version was unveiled in August 2012, using battery pack, electronics, and powertrain components from the Tesla Model S sedan (also launched in 2012).[^cite_note-322] The RAV4 EV had a limited production run which resulted in just under 3,000 vehicles being produced, before it was discontinued in 2014.[^cite_note-323][^cite_note-324]

According to Bloomberg News, the partnership between Tesla and Toyota was "marred by clashes between engineers".[^cite_note-Circuited-325] Toyota engineers rejected designs that Tesla had proposed for an enclosure to protect the RAV4 EV's battery pack. Toyota took over responsibility for the enclosure's design and strengthened it. In 2014, Tesla ended up adding a titanium plate to protect the Model S sedan's battery after some debris-related crashes led to cars catching fire.[^cite_note-Circuited-325][^cite_note-model-s-titanium-200] On June 5, 2017, Toyota announced that it had sold all of its shares in Tesla and halted the partnership.[^cite_note-326][^cite_note-327]

#### Mobileye

Initial versions of Autopilot were developed in partnership with [Mobileye](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mobileye) beginning in 2014.[^cite_note-328] Mobileye ended the partnership on July 26, 2016, citing "disagreements about how the technology was deployed".[^cite_note-329]

## Lawsuits and controversies

Main articles: [List of lawsuits involving Tesla, Inc.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_lawsuits_involving_Tesla,_Inc.) and [Criticism of Tesla, Inc.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Criticism_of_Tesla,_Inc.)

### Failed lawsuit alleging unfair review

In 2008, Tesla provided two Roadsters to the British entertainment car-testing television programme *[Top Gear](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Top_Gear_\(2002_TV_series\))* for testing. Top Gear tested the car as a performance sports car—in a format designed for humour and entertainment, not an unbiased review about its intended purpose—and made some criticisms of its use as such, though full of praise for its performance and handling on the track. Tesla was incensed about what it considered unfair criticism. For example, the test affirmed that *in the performance tests they ran* the battery charge would have lasted for 55 miles, a figure that actually came from Tesla. Tesla said that the figure was untrue and libelous as the car's mileage *in normal use* was claimed to be 211, which they sued, and lost.[^cite_note-330][^cite_note-331]

In 2025, when Tesla was the subject of protests and vandalism in response to Elon Musk's political activities with the Trump government, former Top Gear presenter [Jeremy Clarkson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeremy_Clarkson) published gloating remarks, saying that the vandalism was "not funny. But also, it's kinda hilarious. Especially if you're me."[^cite_note-332]

### Sexual harassment

In 2021, seven women came forward with claims of having faced sexual harassment and discrimination while working at Tesla's Fremont factory.[^cite_note-333] They accused the company of facilitating a culture of rampant sexual harassment. The women said they were consistently subjected to catcalling, unwanted advances, unwanted touching, and discrimination while at work. "I was so tired of the unwanted attention and the males gawking at me, I proceeded to create barriers around me just so I could get some relief," Brooks told *[The Washington Post](102226.md)*. "That was something I felt necessary just so I can do my job." Stories range from intimate groping to being called out to the parking lot for sex.[^cite_note-334]

Women feared calling Human Resources for help, as their supervisors were often participants.[^cite_note-Siddiqui-2021b-335] Musk himself is not indicted, but most of the women pressing charges believe their abuse is connected to the behavior of Musk as CEO. They cite his crude remarks about women's bodies, wisecracks about starting a university that abbreviated to "T.IT.S.", and his generally dismissive attitude towards reporting sexual harassment.[^cite_note-336] "What we're addressing for each of the lawsuits is just a shocking pattern of rampant harassment that exists at Tesla," said attorney David A. Lowe.[^cite_note-Siddiqui-2021b-335] In 2017, another woman had accused Tesla of very similar behavior and was subsequently fired. In a statement to the Guardian, Tesla confirmed the company had terminated her employment, saying it had thoroughly investigated the employee's allegations with the help of "a neutral, third-party expert" and concluded her complaints were unmerited.[^cite_note-337]

In May 2022, a California judge ruled that the sexual harassment lawsuit could move to court, rejecting Tesla's request for closed-door arbitration.[^cite_note-338]

### Labor disputes

Main article: [Tesla and unions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tesla_and_unions)

This section is an excerpt from [Elon Musk and trade unions § Tesla](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elon_Musk_and_trade_unions#Tesla).\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Elon_Musk_and_trade_unions\&action=edit)]

Five smiling workers are standing together. They are holding signs that say "union" wrapped in hearts. Behind them, is a concrete stand emblazoned with the Tesla logo.

[Valentine's Day](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Valentine's_Day) union drive at [Giga New York](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gigafactory_New_York) in 2023

[Tesla has had labor disputes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tesla_and_unions) in the United States, Germany and Sweden, including an ongoing [strike](155134.md) in Sweden. Tesla, Inc., an American [electric car](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Electric_car) and [solar panel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Solar_panel) manufacturer, has more than 140,000 workers employed across its global operations as of January 2024.[^cite_note-Elon_Musk_and_trade_unions_10K_2022-339] Tesla CEO [Elon Musk](909036.md) has expressed his [opposition to unions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Union_busting) on [Twitter](9988187.md) (now called X). The [National Labor Relations Board](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Labor_Relations_Board) held that one [tweet](68533868.md) was unlawful, but was overturned by a [federal appeals court](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Court_of_Appeals_for_the_Fifth_Circuit).[^cite_note-Elon_Musk_and_trade_unions_ap20241029-340] All [unionization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Unionization) efforts at the [Tesla Fremont Factory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tesla_Fremont_Factory) and [Gigafactory New York](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gigafactory_New_York) in the United States have been unsuccessful.[^cite_note-Elon_Musk_and_trade_unions_\:13-341] In Germany, [Gigafactory Berlin-Brandenburg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gigafactory_Berlin-Brandenburg) and [Tesla Automation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tesla_Automation) have elected [works councils](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Works_council), but they have not signed [collective bargaining agreements](12728750.md) with the German trade union [IG Metall](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/IG_Metall).[^cite_note-342][^cite_note-343] The Gigafactory Berlin-Brandenburg works council is divided into pro-union and anti-union factions.[^cite_note-Elon_Musk_and_trade_unions_\:15-344] In Sweden, mechanics who are members of the trade union [IF Metall](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/IF_Metall) have been on strike since October 27, 2023, making it the longest strike in Sweden since 1938.[^cite_note-Elon_Musk_and_trade_unions_\:10-345] The strike has since spread, with other [Swedish, Danish and Norwegian unions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Council_of_Nordic_Trade_Unions) calling for [solidarity strikes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Solidarity_action).[^cite_note-Elon_Musk_and_trade_unions_\:0-346]

### Accidents, repairs, and safety violations

In June 2016, the [National Highway Traffic Safety Administration](989530.md) (NHTSA) took issue with Tesla's use of [nondisclosure agreements](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nondisclosure_agreements) (NDAs) regarding customer repairs[^cite_note-347] and, in October 2021, the NHTSA formally asked Tesla to explain its NDA policy regarding customers invited into the FSD Beta.[^cite_note-348] Tesla has used NDAs on multiple occasions with both employees[^cite_note-349] and customers[^cite_note-350] to allegedly prevent possible negative coverage.[^cite_note-351][^cite_note-352]

From 2014 to 2018, Tesla's Fremont Factory had three times as many [Occupational Safety and Health Administration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Occupational_Safety_and_Health_Administration) (OSHA) violations as the ten largest US auto plants combined.[^cite_note-353] An investigation by the [Reveal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reveal_\(podcast\)) podcast alleged that Tesla "failed to report some of its serious injuries on legally mandated reports" to downplay the extent of injuries.[^cite_note-Evans-Perry-2018-354]

In January 2019, former Tesla security manager Sean Gouthro filed a whistleblower complaint alleging that the company had hacked employees' phones and spied on them, while also failing to report illegal activities to the authorities and shareholders.[^cite_note-O'Kane-2019-355][^cite_note-356][^cite_note-357] Several legal cases have revolved around alleged whistleblower retaliation by Tesla. These include the dismissal of Tesla safety official Carlos Ramirez[^cite_note-358][^cite_note-359] and Tesla security employee Karl Hansen.[^cite_note-360] In 2020, the court ordered Hansen's case to [arbitration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arbitration_in_the_United_States).[^cite_note-361] In June 2022, the arbitrator filed an [unopposed motion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Motion_\(legal\)) with the court stating Hansen "has failed to establish the claims… Accordingly, his claims are denied, and he shall take nothing".[^cite_note-362]

The California Civil Rights Department filed a suit in 2022 alleging "a pattern of racial harassment and bias" at the Tesla Fremont factory. As of April 2023, the department is also conducting a probe of the factory based on a 2021 complaint and claims that Tesla has been obstructing the investigation.[^cite_note-363]

According to *Jalopnik*, a 2024 study by *iSeeCars* found that Tesla had the highest fatal crash rate of any automaker in the United States.[^cite_note-364]

A 2025 lawsuit in California alleged that Tesla odometers were falsely exaggerating their readings to prematurely void their warranty.[^cite_note-365]

In 2025 in the US, Tesla faced a new federal investigation into possible safety defects in its FSD system. This followed 44 separate incident reports where Tesla drivers said the system caused them to commit safety violations, such as driving through red traffic lights.[^cite_note-366]

### Fraud allegations

There have been numerous concerns about Tesla's financial reporting. In 2013, *Bloomberg News* questioned whether Tesla's financial reporting violated [Generally Accepted Accounting Principles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Generally_Accepted_Accounting_Principles_\(United_States\)) (GAAP) reporting standards.[^cite_note-Hiltzikh-2013-367] *[Fortune](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fortune_\(magazine\))* accused Tesla in 2016 of using [creative accounting](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Creative_accounting) to show positive cash flow and quarterly profits.[^cite_note-368] In 2018, analysts expressed concerns over Tesla's accounts receivable balance.[^cite_note-369] In September 2019, the SEC questioned Tesla CFO [Zach Kirkhorn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zach_Kirkhorn) about Tesla's warranty reserves and lease accounting.[^cite_note-370] In a letter to his clients, hedge fund manager [David Einhorn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/David_Einhorn_\(hedge_fund_manager\)), whose firm suffered losses from its short position against Tesla that quarter, accused Musk in November 2019 of "significant fraud",[^cite_note-371][^cite_note-372] and publicly questioned Tesla's accounting practices, telling Musk that he was "beginning to wonder whether your accounts receivable exist."[^cite_note-373]

From 2012 to 2014, Tesla earned more than \$295 million in [Zero Emission Vehicle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zero_Emission_Vehicle) credits for a battery-swapping technology that was never made available to customers.[^cite_note-374] Staff at [California Air Resources Board](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/California_Air_Resources_Board) were concerned that Tesla was "gaming" the battery swap subsidies and in 2013 recommended eliminating the credits.[^cite_note-375]

A consolidated shareholders lawsuit alleges that Musk knew SolarCity was going broke before the acquisition, that he and the Tesla board overpaid for SolarCity, ignored their conflicts of interest and breached their fiduciary duties in connection with the deal, and failed to disclose "troubling facts" essential to an analysis of the proposed acquisition.[^cite_note-Mitchell-2019-376] The members of the board settled in 2020, leaving Musk as the only defendant.[^cite_note-reuters_solarcitylawsuit_2020-377] In April 2022, the [Delaware Court of Chancery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delaware_Court_of_Chancery) ruled in favor of Musk,[^cite_note-378][^cite_note-379] and its ruling was upheld by the [Delaware Supreme Court](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delaware_Supreme_Court) in June 2023.[^cite_note-380]

In August 2018, Musk tweeted, "Am considering taking Tesla private at \$420. Funding secured."[^cite_note-381] The tweet caused the stock to initially rise, but then drop when it was revealed to be false.[^cite_note-382][^cite_note-383][^cite_note-Mitchell-2020-384] Musk settled fraud charges with the US [Securities and Exchange Commission](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Securities_and_Exchange_Commission) (SEC) over his false statements in September 2018. According to the terms of the settlement, Musk agreed to have his tweets reviewed by Tesla's in-house counsel, he was removed from his chairman role at Tesla temporarily, and two new independent directors were appointed to the company's board.[^cite_note-secured-385] Tesla and Musk also paid civil penalties of \$20 million each.[^cite_note-secured-385] A civil [class-action](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Class-action) shareholder lawsuit over Musk's statements and other derivative lawsuits were also filed against Musk and the members of Tesla's board of directors, as then constituted, regarding claims and actions made that were associated with potentially going private.[^cite_note-386][^cite_note-SEC_file-387] In February 2023, a California jury unanimously found Musk and Tesla not liable in the class-action lawsuit.[^cite_note-388]

In September 2018, Tesla disclosed that it was under investigation by the US [Federal Bureau of Investigation](11127.md) (FBI) regarding its Model 3 production figures.[^cite_note-Viswanatha-2018-389] Authorities were investigating whether the company misled investors and made projections about its Model 3 production that it knew would be impossible to meet.[^cite_note-Viswanatha-2018-389] A stockholder class action lawsuit against Tesla related to Model 3 production numbers (unrelated to the FBI investigation) was dismissed in March 2019.[^cite_note-390][^cite_note-391][^cite_note-392]

In May 2024, Reuters reported that US federal prosecutors were investigating whether the company committed securities or wire fraud by "misleading investors and consumers" about Autopilot and Full Self-Driving.[^cite_note-393]

### Tesla US dealership disputes

See also: [Tesla US dealership disputes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tesla_US_dealership_disputes)

Unlike other automakers, Tesla does not rely on franchised [auto dealerships](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Auto_dealership) to sell vehicles and instead directly sells vehicles through its website and a network of company-owned stores. In some areas, Tesla operates locations called "galleries" which "educate and inform customers about our products, but such locations do not actually transact in the sale of vehicles."[^cite_note-10K_2021-182] This is because some jurisdictions, particularly in the United States, prohibit auto manufacturers from directly selling vehicles to consumers. Dealership associations have filed lawsuits to prevent direct sales. These associations argued that the franchise system protects consumers by encouraging dealers to compete, lowering the price a customer pays. They also claimed that direct sales would allow manufacturers to undersell their dealers.[^cite_note-bypass-185] The United States [Federal Trade Commission](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Federal_Trade_Commission) ultimately disproved the associations' claims and recommended allowing direct manufacturer sale, which they concluded would save consumers 8% in average vehicle price.[^cite_note-Lao-Feinstein-2015-394][^cite_note-Read-2015-395][^cite_note-Bodisch-2009-396]

Tesla has also lobbied state governments for the right to directly sell cars.[^cite_note-TechCrunch-2021-397] The company has argued that directly operating stores improves consumer education about electric vehicles,[^cite_note-10K_2021-182] because dealerships would sell both Tesla and gas-powered vehicles. Doing this, according to the company, would then set up a [conflict of interest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Conflict_of_interest) for the dealers since properly advertising the benefits of an electric car would disparage the gas-powered vehicles, creating a disincentive to dealership EV sales.[^cite_note-bypass-185] Musk himself further contended that dealers would have a disincentive to sell electric vehicles because they require less maintenance and therefore would reduce after-sales service revenue, a large profit center for most dealerships.[^cite_note-Yarrow_2014-150]

### Intellectual property

Tesla has sued former employees for stealing company information, including those who left to work for a rival such as [XPeng](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/XPeng) and [Zoox](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zoox_\(company\)).[^cite_note-398] For example, Guangzhi Cao, a Tesla engineer, was accused of uploading [Tesla Autopilot](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tesla_Autopilot) source code to his [iCloud](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ICloud) account,[^cite_note-399] and Alex Khatilov was accused of downloading files related to its Warp Drive software to his personal [Dropbox](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dropbox) account.[^cite_note-400]

### Misappropriation

In 2018, a [class action](7200.md) was filed against Musk and the members of Tesla's board, alleging they breached their [fiduciary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fiduciary) duties by approving Musk's stock-based compensation plan.[^cite_note-SEC_file-387] Musk received the first portion of his stock options payout, worth more than \$700 million, in May 2020.[^cite_note-401]

In July 2023, Tesla board members returned \$735 million to the company to settle a claim from a 2020 lawsuit alleging misappropriation of 11 million stock options granted to Elon Musk, Kimbal Musk, Larry Ellison, and others from 2017 to 2020.[^cite_note-402]

### Environmental violations

In 2019, the [United States Environmental Protection Agency](58666.md) fined Tesla for hazardous waste violations that occurred in 2017.[^cite_note-403] In June 2019, Tesla began negotiating penalties for 19 environmental violations from the [Bay Area Air Quality Management District](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bay_Area_Air_Quality_Management_District);[^cite_note-404] the violations took place around Tesla Fremont's paint shop, where there had been at least four fires between 2014 and 2019.[^cite_note-Niedermeyer-2019-405] Environmental violations and permit deviations at Tesla's Fremont Factory increased from 2018 to 2019 with the production ramp of the Model 3.[^cite_note-406]

In June 2018, Tesla employee [Martin Tripp](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Martin_Tripp) leaked information that Tesla was scrapping or reworking up to 40% of its raw materials at the Nevada Gigafactory.[^cite_note-Robinson-2019-407] After Tesla fired him for the leak, Tripp filed a lawsuit and claimed Tesla's security team gave police a false tip that he was planning a mass shooting at the Nevada factory.[^cite_note-408][^cite_note-O'Kane-2019-355] The court ruled in Tesla's favor on September 17, 2020.[^cite_note-409][^cite_note-410]

In January 2024, 25 California counties sued Tesla, accusing the company of violating state health and safety codes by illegally disposing of hazardous waste. Later that week, the case was settled on the conditions that Tesla pay US\$1.5 million and admit to acting "intentionally" and "negligently". Moreover, Tesla also agreed to train its employees on hazardous waste disposal and to have 10 percent of Tesla's facilities audited for waste disposal for the next 5 years.[^cite_note-411][^cite_note-412][^cite_note-413]

### Property damage

In August 2019, [Walmart](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Walmart) filed a multi-million-dollar lawsuit against Tesla, claiming that Tesla's "negligent installation and maintenance" of solar panels caused roof fires at seven Walmart stores dating back to 2012.[^cite_note-414] Walmart settled with Tesla in November 2019; the terms of the settlement were not disclosed.[^cite_note-415]

In April 2021, a Norwegian judge found Tesla guilty of throttling charging speed through a 2019 over-the-air software update, after they failed to respond to the lawsuit. The 30 customers who were part of the lawsuit were awarded 136,000 Norwegian kroner each (\$16,000).[^cite_note-Porterfield-2021-416][^cite_note-417]

### Racism

Tesla has faced numerous complaints regarding workplace harassment and racial discrimination,[^cite_note-NYTimes-30Nov18-418][^cite_note-419] with one former Tesla worker who attempted to sue the employer describing it as "a hotbed of racist behavior."[^cite_note-420] As of December 2021, three percent of leadership at the company were African-American.[^cite_note-421] A former Black worker described the work environment at Tesla's [Buffalo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Buffalo,_New_York) plant as a "very racist place."[^cite_note-422] Tesla and SpaceX's treatment of [Juneteenth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Juneteenth) in 2020 also came under fire.[^cite_note-423] Approximately 100 former employees have submitted signed statements alleging that the company discriminates specifically against African Americans and "allows a racist environment in its factories."[^cite_note-424]

Few of these cases against Tesla ever make it to trial as most employees are made to sign [arbitration agreements](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arbitration).[^cite_note-425] Employees are afterwards required to resolve such disputes out of court, and behind closed doors.

#### Fremont, California, plant

According to the state's Department of Fair Employment and Housing, the [Fremont](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fremont,_California) factory is a racially segregated place where Black employees claim they are given the most menial[^cite_note-NYTimes-30Nov18-418] and physically demanding work.[^cite_note-Wiessner-2022-426] The accusations of racism culminated in February 2022 with the California Department of Fair Employment and Housing suing Tesla for "discriminating against its Black workers."[^cite_note-427]

In July 2021, former employee Melvin Berry received \$1 million in his discrimination case in arbitration against the company after he claimed he was referred to by the [n-word](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/N-word) and forced to work longer hours at the Fremont plant.[^cite_note-428]

In October 2021, a jury verdict in the *[Owen Diaz vs. Tesla](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Owen_Diaz_vs._Tesla)* trial awarded the plaintiff \$137 million in damages after he had faced racial harassment at Tesla's Fremont facility during 2015–2016.[^cite_note-429][^cite_note-430] In a blog, Tesla stressed that Diaz was never "really" a Tesla worker, and that most uttering of the n-word were expressed in a friendly manner.[^cite_note-431][^cite_note-Kolodny-2021-432] In April 2022, federal judge [William Orrick](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/William_Orrick_III) upheld the jury finding of Tesla's liability but reduced the total damage down to \$15 million.[^cite_note-433] Diaz was given a two-week deadline to decide if he would collect the damages. In June 2022, Diaz announced that he would be rejecting the \$15 million award, opening the door for a new trial.[^cite_note-434] In April 2023, Diaz was awarded \$3.2 million in the new trial.[^cite_note-435]

### COVID-19 pandemic

Tesla's initial response to the [COVID-19 pandemic in the United States](63136490.md) has been the subject of considerable criticism. Musk had sought to exempt the Tesla Fremont factory in Alameda County, California, from the government's stay-at-home orders. In an [earnings call](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Earnings_call) in April, he was heard calling the public health orders "fascist".[^cite_note-Siddiqui-2021a-436] He had also called the public's response to the pandemic "dumb" and had said online that there would be zero cases by April.[^cite_note-437] In May 2020, while [Alameda County](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alameda_County) officials were negotiating with the company to reopen the [Fremont Factory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fremont_Factory) on the 18th, Musk defied local government orders by restarting production on the 11th.[^cite_note-438][^cite_note-439][^cite_note-\:4-440] Tesla also sued Alameda County, questioning the legality of the orders, but backed down after the Fremont Factory was given approval to reopen.[^cite_note-441][^cite_note-cnbc20200613-442] In June 2020, Tesla published a detailed plan for bringing employees back to work and keeping them safe,[^cite_note-443] However, some employees still expressed concern for their health.[^cite_note-cnbc20200612-444]

In May 2020, Musk told workers that they could stay home if they felt uncomfortable coming back to work.[^cite_note-445] But in June, Tesla dismissed an employee who criticized the company for taking inadequate safety measures to protect workers from the coronavirus at the Fremont Factory.[^cite_note-446] Three more employees at Tesla's Fremont Factory claimed they were laid off for staying home out of fear of catching COVID-19. This was subsequently denied by Tesla, which even stated that the employees were still on the payroll.[^cite_note-447] COVID-19 cases at the factory grew from 10 in May 2020 to 125 in December 2020, with about 450 total cases in that time out of the approximately 10,000 workers at the plant (4.5%).[^cite_note-Siddiqui-2021a-436][^cite_note-448]

In China, Tesla had what one executive described as "not a green light from the government to get back to work – but a flashing-sirens police escort."[^cite_note-Campbell-2021a-449] Tesla enjoyed special treatment and strong government support in China, including [tax breaks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tax_break), cheap financing, and assistance in building its [Giga Shanghai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Giga_Shanghai) factory at breakneck speeds.[^cite_note-Campbell-2021a-449] Musk has praised China's way of doing things, a controversial stance due to deteriorating [US–Chinese relations](277880.md#Contemporary_relations), the [persecution of Uyghurs in China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Persecution_of_Uyghurs_in_China), and alleged human rights abuses in Hong Kong.[^cite_note-Campbell-2021a-449]

### Right to repair

In March 2023, a class action antitrust lawsuit was filed against Tesla by Virginia M. Lambrix in San Francisco, alleging that the company unlawfully monopolized the market for maintenance and repair of its vehicles in violation of the [Sherman Act](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sherman_Act) and California [antitrust law](92025.md), as a result of which owners were "forced to pay supracompetitive prices and suffer exorbitant wait times" for maintenance services and repair parts.[^cite_note-450][^cite_note-451] The lawsuit was later combined with four other similar suits.[^cite_note-\:02-452][^cite_note-\:13-453]

While six out of eight alleged antitrust violations were dismissed, in June 2024 US District Judge [Trina Thompson](69183886.md) allowed two claims to proceed, including alleged violations of California's Cartwright Act and Unfair Competition Law (UCL), with the court finding evidence of a repairs monopoly in Tesla's designing of its vehicles to require diagnostic and software updates that only the company could provide, and evidence of a parts monopoly in Tesla's restricting [original equipment manufacturers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Original_equipment_manufacturer) from selling "to anyone other than Tesla."[^cite_note-\:02-452][^cite_note-\:13-453]

### TeslaTakedown protests

Main article: [Tesla Takedown](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tesla_Takedown)

In February 2025, numerous planned protests occurred outside of Tesla showrooms and service centers throughout the US. The protests were in response to Musk's contentious actions as leader of the [Department of Government Efficiency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Department_of_Government_Efficiency) (DOGE) at the beginning of Donald Trump's second term as US president.[^cite_note-454][^cite_note-455]

### Lawsuits against Tesla critics and vehicle malfunction complainants in China

On February 12, 2025, the [Associated Press](18935732.md) reported that in China, Tesla had sued six car owners, six or more bloggers, and two media outlets for [defamation](28661.md) since 2021. The car owners had complained publicly about Tesla's quality and accidents caused by major mechanical malfunctions, like brake failure. The bloggers and media outlets had written critically about the company. As of the date of publication, the Associated Press reported that Tesla had won 11 verdicts, two judgments were on appeal, and one had been settled outside court.[^cite_note-456]

## Criticism

Main article: [Criticism of Tesla, Inc.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Criticism_of_Tesla,_Inc.)

### Data privacy

In September 2023, a Tesla vehicle was the second product ever reviewed by the [Mozilla Foundation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mozilla_Foundation) that failed all of their privacy criteria.[^cite_note-457][^cite_note-458]

A Tesla owner filed a lawsuit in 2023 following a [Reuters](18998750.md) report that Tesla employees shared "highly invasive videos and images recorded by customers' car cameras" with one another.[^cite_note-459]

Internal data troves shared with various international government agencies and news organizations by former employee and [whistleblower](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Whistleblower) Lukasz Krupski in late 2023 implicated Tesla in "serious data protection lapse\[s]."[^cite_note-460] The data Krupski retrieved included "information about current and former Tesla staff, including passport numbers, medical details and salaries" and was readily available on internal systems that most employees had access to.[^cite_note-461] As of November 2023, the Data Protection Authority in the Netherlands was investigating whether Tesla's alleged lack of internal security violated privacy laws.[^cite_note-462]

### Short sellers

[TSLAQ](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/TSLAQ) is a collective of Tesla skeptics and [short-sellers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Short-sellers) who are vocally critical of Tesla and aim to "shape \[the] perception \[of Tesla] and move its stock."[^cite_note-463] In January 2020, 20% of Tesla stock was shorted, the highest at that time of any stock in the US equity markets.[^cite_note-464] By early 2021, according to CNN, short sellers had lost \$40 billion during 2020 as the stock price climbed much higher.[^cite_note-465] [Michael Burry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michael_Burry), a short seller portrayed in *[The Big Short](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Big_Short_\(film\))*, had shorted Tesla previously via his firm [Scion Asset Management](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Scion_Asset_Management), but removed his position in October 2021.[^cite_note-466]

### Tesla's mission

According to automotive journalist Jamie Kitman, when multiple CEOs of major automotive manufacturers approached Tesla for EV technology that Musk had claimed the company was willing to share, they instead were offered the opportunity to buy regulatory credits from the company. This suggested, according to Kitman, that "the company may not be as eager for the electric revolution to occur as it claims."[^cite_note-467]

### Giga New York audit

In 2020, the [New York State Comptroller](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_York_State_Comptroller) released an audit of the [Giga New York](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Giga_New_York) factory project, concluding that it presented many red flags, including a lack of basic [due diligence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Due_diligence) and that the factory itself produced only \$0.54 in economic benefits for every \$1 spent by the state.[^cite_note-468][^cite_note-469][^cite_note-470]

### Delays

Musk has been criticized for repeatedly pushing out both production and release dates of products.[^cite_note-471][^cite_note-472] By one count in 2016, Musk had missed 20 projections.[^cite_note-473] In October 2017, Musk predicted that Model 3 production would be 5,000 units per week by December.[^cite_note-474] A month later, he revised that target to "sometime in March" 2018.[^cite_note-WP_production-hell-475] Delivery dates for the Model 3 were delayed as well.[^cite_note-476] Other projects like converting supercharger stations to be [solar-powered](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Solar-power) have also lagged projections.[^cite_note-477] Musk responded in late 2018: "punctuality is not my strong suit...I never made a mass-produced car. How am I supposed to know with precision when it's gonna get done?"[^cite_note-478]

## Accolades

In 2019, Model 3 earned the Top Safety Pick+ Award from the [Insurance Institute for Highway Safety](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Insurance_Institute_for_Highway_Safety).[^cite_note-479] In 2022, Model Y has received the highest safety score of any car analyzed under [Euro NCAP](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Euro_NCAP).[^cite_note-480]

In 2022, Tesla won [S\&P Global](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/S\&P_Global) Automotive Loyalty Awards in 7 categories.[^cite_note-481]

In 2025, [TIME](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/TIME) magazine named Model S as one of the best inventions of the past 25 years, praising it as a "game-changing advance for electric vehicles".[^cite_note-482]

*[Motor Trend](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Motor_Trend)* has shortlisted Cybertruck for the magazine's 2025 Truck of the Year, praising its [steer-by-wire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Steer-by-wire) system and other tech, but criticizing "the design's many compromises".[^cite_note-483]

## Vehicle product issues

### Recalls

On April 20, 2017, Tesla issued a worldwide recall of 53,000 (\~70%) of the 76,000 vehicles it sold in 2016 due to faulty parking brakes which could become stuck and "prevent the vehicles from moving".[^cite_note-484][^cite_note-485] On March 29, 2018, Tesla issued a worldwide recall of 123,000 Model S cars built before April 2016 due to [corrosion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Corrosion)-susceptible [power steering](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Power_steering) bolts, which could fail and require the driver to use "increased force" to control the vehicle.[^cite_note-486]

In October 2020, Tesla initiated a recall of nearly 50,000 Model X and Y vehicles throughout China for suspension issues.[^cite_note-487] Soon after in November, the [NHTSA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NHTSA) announced it had opened its investigation into 115,000 Tesla cars regarding "front suspension safety issues", citing specifically 2015–2017 Model S and 2016–2017 Model X years. Cases of the "whompy wheel" phenomenon, which also included Model X and the occasional Model 3 cars, have been documented through 2020.[^cite_note-488][^cite_note-489]

In February 2021, Tesla was required by the NHTSA to recall 135,000 Model S and Model X vehicles built from 2012 to 2018 due to using a [flash memory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Flash_memory) device that was rated to last only 5 to 6 years.[^cite_note-490] The problem was related to touchscreen failures that could possibly affect the rearview camera, safety systems, Autopilot and other features.[^cite_note-491][^cite_note-492] The underlying technical reason is that the car writes a large amount of [syslog](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Syslog) content to the device, wearing it out prematurely.[^cite_note-493]

Also in February 2021, the German Federal Motor Transport Authority (KBA) ordered Tesla to recall 12,300 Model X cars because of "body mouldings problems".[^cite_note-494][^cite_note-495]

In June 2021, Tesla recalled 5,974 electric vehicles due to worries that brake caliper bolts might become loose, which could lead to loss of tire pressure, increasing the chance of a crash.[^cite_note-496]

On December 30, 2021, Tesla announced that it is recalling more than 475,000 US model vehicles. This included 356,309 Model 3 Tesla vehicles from 2017 to 2020 due to rear-view camera issues and a further 119,009 Tesla Model S vehicles due to potential problems with the trunk or boot. The Model S recall includes vehicles manufactured between 2014 and 2021. Around 1% of recalled Model 3s may have a defective rear-view camera, and around 14% of recalled Model S may have the defect. The recall was not linked to a contemporaneous issue regarding the "Passenger Play" feature, which allowed games to be played on the touchscreen while the car is in motion.[^cite_note-497] After an investigation was launched by the NHTSA covering 585,000 vehicles, Tesla agreed to make changes where the feature would be locked and unusable while the car is moving.[^cite_note-498]

In September 2022, Tesla announced that it is recalling almost 1.1 million US model vehicles because the automatic window reversal system might not react correctly after detecting an obstruction, increasing the risk of injury.[^cite_note-499][^cite_note-Root-2022-500] In response, Tesla announced an over-the-air software fix.[^cite_note-Root-2022-500]

In February 2023, Tesla recalled its FSD software following a recommendation from NHTSA; the recall applied to approximately 360,000 cars.[^cite_note-501] NHTSA found that FSD caused "unreasonable risk" when used on city streets.[^cite_note-502] In March 2023, about 3,500 Model Y Teslas were recalled for a bolting issue concerning the cars' second-row seats.[^cite_note-503]

In December 2023, following a 2-year investigation by the [NHTSA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NHTSA),[^cite_note-504] Tesla issued a wider recall on all vehicles equipped with any version of Autosteer, including 2012–2023 Model S; 2016–2023 Model X; 2017–2023 Model 3; and 2020–2023 Model Y, covering 2,031,220 vehicles in total.[^cite_note-NHTSA_23V-838-505] The NHTSA concluded that Autosteer's controls were not sufficient to prevent misuse and did not ensure that the drivers maintained "continuous and sustained responsibility for vehicle operation" and states that affected vehicles will receive an over-the-air software remedy.[^cite_note-NHTSA_23V-838-505][^cite_note-506]

### Electrically operated door handles

In August 2026, Tesla began recalling vehicles in China due to difficulty escaping vehicles in a life-threatening situations.[^cite_note-507][^cite_note-\:7-508][^cite_note-\:8-509] Electrically operated door handles in the cars may fail to open after an emergency, and the cars lack a clear manual release.[^cite_note-\:7-508][^cite_note-\:8-509] The recall will involve adding a warning label on the interior door and a software update to automatically lower the windows in a crash.[^cite_note-\:7-508][^cite_note-\:8-509]

Starting January 1, 2027, China will also require a mechanical release on both the inside and outside of every door except the trunk, and Tesla will have to modify its door design to comply.[^cite_note-\:9-510][^cite_note-511][^cite_note-\:9-510][^cite_note-512][^cite_note-513] Regulators in other countries are also investigating the issue.[^cite_note-514][^cite_note-515]

At least 15 people have died in the United States in Teslas where the vehicle's doors would not open following a crash.[^cite_note-516][^cite_note-auto2-517] Tesla vehicles use electrically operated door handles with a concealed manual release intended for use when electrical power is unavailable; however the back seat doors in some Model 3 and Model Y vehicles do not have manual releases.[^cite_note-auto2-517]

### Fires

See also: [Plug-in electric vehicle fire incidents § Tesla](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Plug-in_electric_vehicle_fire_incidents#Tesla)

Tesla customers have reported the company as being "slow" to address how its cars can ignite.[^cite_note-518] In 2013, a Model S caught fire after the vehicle hit metal debris on a highway in [Kent, Washington](138005.md). Tesla confirmed the fire began in the battery pack and was caused by the impact of an object.[^cite_note-519] As a result of this and other incidents, Tesla announced its decision to extend its current vehicle warranty to cover fire damage.[^cite_note-520] In March 2014, the NHTSA announced that it had closed the investigation into whether the Model S was prone to catch fire, after Tesla said it would provide more protection to its battery packs.[^cite_note-521] All Model S cars manufactured after March 6, 2014, have had the 0.25-inch (6.4 mm) aluminum shield over the battery pack replaced with a new three-layer shield.[^cite_note-522] In October 2019, the NHTSA opened an investigation into possible battery defects in Tesla's Model S and X vehicles from 2012 to 2019 that could cause "non-crash" fires.[^cite_note-523][^cite_note-524][^cite_note-525]

### Autopilot crashes

See also: [List of Tesla Autopilot crashes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Tesla_Autopilot_crashes)

A Model S driver died in a collision with a tractor-trailer in 2016, while the vehicle was in Autopilot mode; the driver is believed to be the first person to have died in a Tesla vehicle in Autopilot.[^cite_note-526][^cite_note-527] The NHTSA investigated the accident but found no safety-related defect trend.[^cite_note-528] In March 2018, a driver of a Tesla Model X was killed in a crash. Investigators say that the driver of the vehicle had his car in 'self-driving' mode and was using his phone to play games when the vehicle collided with the barrier in the middle of the freeway. Through investigation, the NTSB found that the Tesla malfunctioned due to the system being confused by an exit on the freeway.[^cite_note-529]

According to a document released in June 2021, the NHTSA has initiated at least 30 investigations into Tesla crashes that were believed to involve the use of Autopilot, with some [involving fatalities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tesla_Autopilot#Fatal_crashes).[^cite_note-530][^cite_note-531] In early September 2021, the NHTSA updated the list with an additional fatality incident[^cite_note-auto-532] and ordered Tesla to hand over all extensive data pertaining to US cars with Autopilot to determine if there is a safety defect that leads Tesla cars to collide with first-responder vehicles.[^cite_note-auto-532][^cite_note-533][^cite_note-534] In late September 2021, Tesla released an over-the-air software update to detect emergency lights at night.[^cite_note-535] In October 2021, the NHTSA asked Tesla why it did not issue a recall when it sent out that update.[^cite_note-536] In June 2022, the NHTSA said it would expand its probe, extending it to 830,000 cars from all current Tesla models. The probe was moved up from the *Preliminary Evaluation* level to *Engineering Analysis*. The regulator cited the reason for the expansion as the need to "explore the degree to which Autopilot and associated Tesla systems may exacerbate human factors or behavioral safety risks by undermining the effectiveness of the driver's supervision."[^cite_note-537]

A safety test conducted by the Dawn Project in August 2022 demonstrated that a test driver using the beta version of Full Self-Driving repeatedly hit a child-sized mannequin in its path,[^cite_note-538] but there has been controversy over its conclusions.[^cite_note-539] Several Tesla owners responded by conducting their own, independent tests using children; NHTSA released a statement warning against the practice.[^cite_note-540]

In 2025, the California DMV filed a lawsuit against Tesla, claiming it had misled drivers about its vehicles' self-driving capabilities. The DMV sought to suspend Tesla's sales and manufacturing in the state of California for a minimum of 30 days.[^cite_note-541][^cite_note-542]

### Software hacking

In August 2015, two researchers said they were able to take control of a Tesla Model S by hacking into the car's entertainment system.[^cite_note-543] The hack required the researchers to physically access the car.[^cite_note-544] Tesla issued a [security update](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Patch_\(computing\)) for the Model S the day after the exploit was announced.[^cite_note-545]

In September 2016, researchers at [Tencent](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tencent)'s Keen Security Lab demonstrated a remote attack on a Tesla Model S and controlled the vehicle in both Parking and Driving Mode without physical access. They were able to compromise the automotive networking bus ([CAN bus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CAN_bus)) when the vehicle's web browser was used while the vehicle was connected to a malicious Wi-Fi hotspot.[^cite_note-546] This was the first case of a remote control exploit demonstrated on a Tesla. The vulnerability was disclosed to Tesla under its [bug bounty program](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bug_bounty_program) and patched within 10 days, before the exploit was made public.[^cite_note-547] Tencent also hacked the doors of a Model X in 2017.[^cite_note-548]

In January 2018, security researchers informed Tesla that an [Amazon Web Services](1691376.md) account of theirs could be accessed directly from the Internet and that the account had been exploited for [cryptocurrency mining](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cryptocurrency_mining). Tesla responded by securing the compromised system, rewarding the security researchers financially via its bug bounty program, and stating that the compromise did not violate customer privacy, nor vehicle safety or security.[^cite_note-549][^cite_note-550] Later in 2019, Tesla awarded a car and \$375,000 to [ethical hackers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/White_hat_\(computer_security\)) during a [Pwn2Own](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pwn2Own) Model 3 hacking event.[^cite_note-551]

In June 2022, Martin Herfurt, a security researcher in Austria, discovered that changes made to make Tesla vehicles easier to start with [NFC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Near-field_communication) cards also allowed for pairing new keys to the vehicle, allowing an attacker to enroll their keys to a vehicle.[^cite_note-552]

### Phantom braking

In February 2022, Tesla drivers reported a surge in "phantom braking" events when using Tesla Autopilot, which coincides with the automaker's removal of radar as a supplemental sensor in May 2021.[^cite_note-553] In response, NHTSA opened an investigation the same month.[^cite_note-554] In August 2022, a consumer class action was filed alleging that the Autopilot system in Tesla cars "contains a hazardous defect which causes the vehicle to suddenly and unintentionally brake".[^cite_note-555][^cite_note-556] In November 2024, Tesla failed to persuade a [US District Judge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/US_District_Judge) to dismiss the lawsuit.[^cite_note-\:0-557] The next court date is January 7, 2025.[^cite_note-\:0-557]

In May 2023, German business newspaper *[Handelsblatt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Handelsblatt)* published a series of articles based on a trove of internal Tesla data submitted to them from informants.[^cite_note-558] The 100 gigabytes of data "contain\[ed] over 1,000 accident reports involving phantom braking or unintended acceleration" as well as complaints about Tesla Autopilot.[^cite_note-559] [Dutch authorities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dutch_Data_Protection_Authority) responded by saying they were investigating the company for possible data privacy violations.[^cite_note-560]

In February 2025, a German court declared Tesla Autopilot defective for normal use due to phantom braking issues.[^cite_note-561]

In February 2025, a group of Australian buyers sued Tesla with a [class action](7200.md) over multiple issues like battery range, driving assistance, and phantom braking.[^cite_note-562]

In July 2026, the [NHTSA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NHTSA) ended their Tesla Phantom Braking probe as customer complaints fell.[^cite_note-563]

### Driving range performance

Tesla has received thousands of complaints from owners that the driving ranges of their vehicles did not meet the ranges advertised by Tesla or the projections of in-dash range meters. When service centers were overwhelmed with appointments to take care of these issues, Tesla established a diversion team to cancel as many appointments as possible. Customers were told that remote diagnostics had determined there was no problem, and their appointments were canceled. The company has been fined by South Korean regulators for its exaggerated range estimates.[^cite_note-564]

### Suspension failure risks

In 2026 the [National Highway Traffic Safety Administration](989530.md) opened a preliminary investigation into Tesla over complaints of a suspension failure risk in 2018-2020 Model 3's and 2021-2023 ​Model Y's, at the time of investigation no crashes, injuries or fatalities have been reported.[^cite_note-565]

## Vehicle sales

In 2024, Tesla ranked as the world's best-selling [battery electric](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battery_electric_vehicle) passenger car manufacturer, with a market share of 17.6%.[^cite_note-566] Tesla reported 2023 vehicle deliveries of 1.8 million units, up 38% from 2022.[^cite_note-567][^cite_note-568] In March 2024, Tesla produced its six millionth car.[^cite_note-569] In Q4 2023, Tesla's Chinese competitor [BYD](1461760.md) took over the top spot for EVs shipped, but Tesla regained the title in Q1 2024.[^cite_note-570] On January 2, 2026, BYD became the bestselling electric vehicle maker across the globe and surpassed Tesla as the largest EV maker.[^cite_note-571]

Tesla sells the most vehicles in the US (674,000+ in 2023) and has received \$11.4 billion in regulatory credits from federal and state governments. The federal government previously provided a \$7500 rebate to customers.[^cite_note-572][^cite_note-573][^cite_note-574] Tesla received around \$426 million from the Chinese government for cars manufactured at its Shanghai facility.[^cite_note-575] Germany was Tesla's third-largest market in 2023, selling 63,682 units. Germany ended its EV subsidy program in December 2023. Under the program, a €6,750 discount was provided with Tesla paying €2,250. Tesla announced it would cover the entire discount going forward.[^cite_note-576] In 2023, over 63,000 Teslas were sold in France with customers receiving up to €7,000 via the bonus écologique. The government decreased the budget for EV aid from €1.5 billion to €700 million in 2024.[^cite_note-577] Canada was in Tesla's top five markets in 2023 with over 52,000 vehicles sold. In 2025, Tesla was accused of having "a run on the bank" when it sold a historic 8,600 vehicles in 72 hours before [Canada's EV rebate program](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Government_incentives_for_plug-in_electric_vehicles#Canada)'s funds were depleted. Tesla claimed 4000 vehicles alone were sold over one weekend in Quebec City.[^cite_note-578][^cite_note-579] The Government of Canada announced on March 25, 2025, that it had suspended the rebates and was investigating. The government also declared Tesla would be ineligible for any future rebates as long as the US tariffs on Canadian products continued.[^cite_note-580] Musk has stated he is against government subsidies but will take them if they are available.[^cite_note-581]

Sales have been negatively impacted by Musk's actions during the [second Trump presidency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Second_Trump_presidency).[^cite_note-582][^cite_note-583] Tesla sales have decreased worldwide (the most in countries like France, Germany, the UK, Sweden, Norway, and the Netherlands)[^cite_note-584][^cite_note-585] with his vocal support for far-right parties such as [Reform UK](59757202.md)[^cite_note-586] and [AfD](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AfD)[^cite_note-587] in Germany along with his [Nazi salute controversy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elon_Musk_salute_controversy) leading to boycotts of his products and Tesla vehicles being nicknamed **Swasticars**, a [portmanteau](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Portmanteau) of *[swastika](45943.md)* and *car*.[^cite_note-594] German sales fell 76% in February 2025 compared to February 2024.[^cite_note-595][^cite_note-596]

Tesla's shares dropped over 9 percent after its sales in the EU and UK declined by nearly half in January 2025, bringing its valuation below \$1 trillion for the first time since November 2024. While European electric car sales surged, Tesla struggled against rising competition, particularly from Chinese automakers.[^cite_note-597] In May 2025, it was reported that the company's sales in Europe declined every month since the start of the year, including in April where sales dropped 49 percent to 7,261 from 14,228 in April 2024.[^cite_note-\:1-598] As of May 2025, the company has a 0.7 percent market share in Europe, down from 1.3 percent a year ago.[^cite_note-\:1-598]

Tesla deliveries vary significantly by month due to regional issues such as availability of [car carriers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Car_carrier) and registration. On March 9, 2020, the company produced its 1 millionth electric car, becoming the first [auto manufacturer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Auto_manufacturer) to achieve such a milestone.[^cite_note-1million-599] In the third quarter of 2021, Tesla sold its 2 millionth electric car, becoming the first auto manufacturer to achieve such a milestone.[^cite_note-600] In the first quarter of 2023, the Model Y became the world's best-selling car, surpassing the Toyota Corolla.[^cite_note-601]

## Finances

&#x20;View [chart definition](https://commons.wikimedia.org/wiki/Data\:Template%20bar.chart).

Tesla financial performance (in Billion USD)

For the fiscal (and calendar) year 2021, Tesla reported a net income of \$5.52 billion.[^cite_note-Tesla4Q2021final-602] The annual revenue was \$53.8 billion, an increase of 71% over the previous fiscal year.[^cite_note-Tesla4Q2021final-602]

| Business | Sales in billion \$ | Share |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Automotive | 87.6 | 89.7% |
| Energy Generation and Storage | 10.1 | 10.3% |

| Region | Sales in billion \$ | Share |
| --- | --- | --- |
| United States | 47.7 | 48.9% |
| Other countries | 29.0 | 29.7% |
| China | 20.9 | 21.4% |

Of the revenue number in 2021, \$314 million came from selling regulatory credits to other automakers to meet government pollution standards. That number has been a smaller percentage of revenue for multiple quarters.[^cite_note-Tesla4Q2021final-602]

Tesla ended 2021 with \$17.6 billion of cash on hand, down \$1.8 billion from the end of 2020.[^cite_note-10K_2021-182]<sup>: 31 </sup>

In February 2021, a [10-K](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Form_10-K) filing revealed that Tesla had invested some \$1.5 billion in the cryptocurrency bitcoin, and the company indicated it would soon accept bitcoin as a form of payment.[^cite_note-bb20210208-88] Critics then pointed out how investing in cryptocurrency can run counter to Tesla's environmental goals.[^cite_note-604][^cite_note-605] Tesla made more profit from the 2021 investment than the profit from selling cars in 2020, due to the Bitcoin price increase after the investment was announced.[^cite_note-606][^cite_note-607]

The quarter ending June 2021 was the first time Tesla made a profit independent of Bitcoin and regulatory credits.[^cite_note-608] Between 2015 and 2024, a third of Tesla's profits came from government climate credits.[^cite_note-609]

### Board of directors

Tesla has received criticism that its board lacks enough independent directors. In an April 2017 public letter, a group of influential Tesla investors, including the [California State Teachers' Retirement System](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/California_State_Teachers'_Retirement_System), asked Tesla to add two new independent directors to its board "who do not have any ties" with Musk,[^cite_note-Independent-615] writing that "five of six current non-executive directors have professional or personal ties to Mr. Musk that could put at risk their ability to exercise independent judgement."[^cite_note-USA-Today-2017-04-12-616] Tesla's directors at the time included Brad Buss, who was chief financial officer at SolarCity; [Steve Jurvetson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Steve_Jurvetson), a venture capitalist who also sits on the board of SpaceX;[^cite_note-BI_BOD_2018-617] Elon Musk's brother, [Kimbal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kimbal); and [Ira Ehrenpreis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ira_Ehrenpreis?action=edit\&redlink=1) and [Antonio Gracias](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antonio_Gracias), both of whom also invested in SpaceX.[^cite_note-618] The letter called for a more independent board that could put a check on [groupthink](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Groupthink).[^cite_note-USA-Today-2017-04-12-616] At first Musk responded on Twitter, writing that the investors "should buy Ford stock" because "their governance is amazing."[^cite_note-USA-Today-2017-04-12-616] Two days later, he promised he would add two independent board members;[^cite_note-619] [Kathleen Wilson-Thompson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kathleen_Wilson-Thompson) and [Larry Ellison](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Larry_Ellison) were added at the end of 2018.[^cite_note-620] Ellison stepped down in August 2022.[^cite_note-621] Former Tesla CTO [J. B. Straubel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/J._B._Straubel), who left the company in 2019, was elected to the board in 2023.[^cite_note-jb-board-622]

Another criticism of the board composition is that most of the independent directors lack automotive industry experience.[^cite_note-623] The exception is Robyn Denholm who had finance and corporate reporting roles at [Toyota Australia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Toyota_Australia) from 1989 to 1996.[^cite_note-cleantech_denholm_2018-624]

Other previous board members include businessman [Steve Westly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Steve_Westly); Daimler executive Herbert Kohler;[^cite_note-TN_BOD_2012-307] CEO and Chairman of [Johnson Publishing Company](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Johnson_Publishing_Company) Linda Johnson Rice;[^cite_note-GTM_BOD_2019-625] and [United Nations Special Envoy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Special_Envoy_of_the_Secretary-General) on Innovative Finance and Sustainable Investments [Hiromichi Mizuno](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hiromichi_Mizuno).[^cite_note-626][^cite_note-627]

As of December 31, 2025, the board members are:[^cite_note-\:10-628]

| Joined | Name | Titles | [Independent](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Independent_director) | Ref. |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 2014 | [Robyn Denholm](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Robyn_Denholm) | Chair (since November 2018); former CFO and Head of Strategy at [Telstra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Telstra) | Yes | [^cite_note-629][^cite_note-cleantech_denholm_2018-624] |
| 2004 | [Elon Musk](909036.md) | CEO, [product architect](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chief_product_officer), former chair; founder, CEO and CTO of SpaceX | No | [^cite_note-FOOTNOTEVance2015155-19] |
| 2004 | [Kimbal Musk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kimbal_Musk) | SpaceX board member | No | [^cite_note-630][^cite_note-631] |
| 2007 | [Ira Ehrenpreis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ira_Ehrenpreis?action=edit\&redlink=1) | General Partner at Technology Partners | Disputed | [^cite_note-632][^cite_note-GTM_BOD_2019-625][^cite_note-Independent-615] |
| 2017 | [James Murdoch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Murdoch) | Former CEO of [21st Century Fox](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/21st_Century_Fox) | Yes | [^cite_note-GTM_BOD_2019-625] |
| 2018 | [Kathleen Wilson-Thompson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kathleen_Wilson-Thompson) | Global head of Human Resources of [Walgreens Boots Alliance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Walgreens_Boots_Alliance) | Yes | [^cite_note-BI_BOD_2018-617] |
| 2022 | [Joe Gebbia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joe_Gebbia) | Co-founder, board member, and advisor of [Airbnb](32058290.md) | Yes | [^cite_note-633][^cite_note-634] |
| 2023 | [J. B. Straubel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/J._B._Straubel) | Founder and CEO of [Redwood Materials](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Redwood_Materials); former CTO of Tesla | Disputed | [^cite_note-jb-board-622][^cite_note-635] |
| 2025 | Jack Hartung | Senior advisor at [Chipotle Mexican Grill](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill). Former president of Chipotle. | Yes | [^cite_note-636][^cite_note-637] |

### Ownership structure

The largest shareholders of Tesla as of December 31, 2025:[^cite_note-\:10-628]

| Shareholder name | Percentage |
| --- | --- |
| [Elon Musk](909036.md) | 20.3% |
| [The Vanguard Group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Vanguard_Group) | 6.1% |
| [BlackRock](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BlackRock) | 5.0% |
| Others | 68.6% |

### Taxes

From 2007 to 2025, except for 2023, Tesla has declared owing no taxes to the US government.[^cite_note-\:2-638] Tesla likely saved \$400 million in US taxes over the years by shifting \$18 billion in profits to Singapore and the Netherlands.[^cite_note-\:2-638]
